Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really appreciate your time. 

Um... well, I hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here, and I certainly don't want to waste your time. It's just that... well, since I stopped working, I can’t seem to slow down. I guess I just like to stay productive, you know? It's not a bad thing to be a hard worker. But I'm up and moving from one little job around the house to the next, just all day long. It's almost like I still have a crew waiting on me to tell them what to do. 

I just can't seem to turn it off now that I'm retired. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.

Well, um... I suppose a little bit. But I think everyone gets a little antsy sometimes, don't you think? 

Like, if I have to sit through a really quiet meeting, or... say, a long talk at church. I do notice I feel like I've got to shift around in my seat a fair amount. Or maybe tap my foot. Sometimes I just get this strong urge to get up and stretch my legs, even when I know perfectly well I should just stay put and listen. 

I always try to keep it subtle, though, so I don't bother the folks sitting next to me. I really don't want to be a nuisance, and I hope that doesn't make me sound disrespectful to the pastor or anything. It's just a little hard for me to settle into a chair for too long. Is that the sort of thing you're asking about, Doctor?

Thank you so much for seeing me, I really appreciate your time. \n\nUm... well, I hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill here, and I certainly don't want to waste your time. But I'm up and moving from one little job around the house to the next, just all day long. It's almost like I still have a crew waiting on me to tell them what to do. \n\nI just can't seem to turn it off now that I'm retired. Does that make sense? Sometimes I just get this strong urge to get up and stretch my legs, even when I know perfectly well I should just stay put and listen. \n\nI always try to keep it subtle, though, so I don't bother the folks sitting next to me. I really don't want to be a nuisance, and I hope that doesn't make me sound disrespectful to the pastor or anything. It's just a little hard for me to settle into a chair for too long. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But I think it's pretty normal to notice things going on around you, isn't it? I mean, I like to be aware of my surroundings. 

But, well, sometimes if I'm... say, trying to paint something, or maybe jot down some ideas for a story I've been thinking about... if I hear a loud truck go by outside, or if I suddenly remember a weed I meant to pull in the garden, my mind just kind of... jumps right over to that. And then it takes me a minute to figure out where I was with my painting. 

It's not a huge problem, really! I always get back to it eventually. I just have a curious mind, I suppose. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm too scatterbrained. Does that sound like what you mean, Doctor?

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness, no. I've always been a fairly good sleeper, thankfully. I mean, at my age, I might wake up a little earlier than I used to, maybe to use the restroom or just because the sun is coming up. But I certainly don't feel like I need *less* sleep. 

If I don't get a good night's rest, I definitely feel it the next day. I get a bit groggy, just like anyone else would. I try to get my solid seven or eight hours, you know, to keep my strength up and stay healthy.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I suppose sometimes I might get a little fidgety, but nothing too terrible, I promise. It's just... well, for example, if I'm sitting through a quiet meeting, or maybe listening to a long talk at church, I do find it a bit tricky to just sit perfectly still. 

I might shift around in my seat a bit, or maybe tap my foot without realizing it. Sometimes I just feel this... this little urge to get up and stretch my legs, even though I know it's not the polite time to do so. I try very hard to be respectful and stay put, of course! 

I think maybe it's just my body wanting to stay active, you know? A lot of folks my age probably get a little stiff sitting for too long, don't you think? It's not a big bother to anyone else, I hope. That's not too unusual, is it, Doctor?

I've always been a fairly good sleeper, thankfully. I mean, at my age, I might wake up a little earlier than I used to, maybe to use the restroom or just because the sun is coming up. But I certainly don't feel like I need *less* sleep. \n\nIf I don't get a good night's rest, I definitely feel it the next day. I get a bit groggy, just like anyone else would. I try to get my solid seven or eight hours, you know, to keep my strength up and stay healthy. \n\nI'm not doing anything strange like staying up all night or running around on two hours of sleep. That just wouldn't be very good for my health, would it? I hope that's the kind of answer you're looking for, Doctor. I like to keep myself occupied so I don't get too bored, but I can definitely sit down and watch a good television program in the evening. I'm not a nervous wreck or anything like that. \n\nI just want to make sure I'm doing things right and not letting the days slip by. That's a pretty normal way to look at things, isn't it, Doctor? I suppose sometimes I might get a little fidgety, but nothing too terrible, I promise. It's just... well, for example, if I'm sitting through a quiet meeting, or maybe listening to a long talk at church, I do find it a bit tricky to just sit perfectly still. \n\nI might shift around in my seat a bit, or maybe tap my foot without realizing it. Sometimes I just feel this... this little urge to get up and stretch my legs, even though I know it's not the polite time to do so. I try very hard to be respectful and stay put, of course! \n\nI think maybe it's just my body wanting to stay active, you know? A lot of folks my age probably get a little stiff sitting for too long, don't you think? It's not a big bother to anyone else, I hope. That's not too unusual, is it, Doctor?\n\n[Last Question]\nHave these feelings or behaviors caused you any real problems in your daily life—like making it hard to enjoy time with family, follow through on plans, or get along with others?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well, um... I wouldn't say they cause *real* problems. Nothing too serious, I promise. I try very hard to be dependable! But... well, I suppose I have needed a little extra help lately. 

My wife, bless her, she’s had to take over keeping our calendar and, you know, sort of checking behind me to make sure things are in order. She’s just so organized, so it makes sense for her to handle it, right? 

And... well, I guess I have had to apologize to the folks at the community center, and my garden group, too. Maybe more than once, if I'm being perfectly honest. Just for, um, letting people down or forgetting things I promised to help with. I always feel just terrible about it when it happens, I really do. I don't want anyone thinking poorly of me, or thinking I don't care. 

But we all need a little help keeping things straight sometimes, don't we, Doctor? I'm still doing okay overall, I hope.

I just try to stay positive and keep busy. Does that help answer your question, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you found yourself getting easily distracted by things in your surroundings, or having trouble staying focused on a task lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? But I think it's pretty normal to notice things going on around you, isn't it? And then it takes me a minute to figure out where I was with my painting. \n\nIt's not a huge problem, really! I always get back to it eventually. I just have a curious mind, I suppose. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm too scatterbrained. Does that sound like what you mean, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you felt down, sad, or experienced a low mood most days, even when things seem to be going well for you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't feel that way at all. I try to look on the bright side of things, you know? \n\nI mean, everyone has a gloomy day here and there, sure, but... no, I wouldn't say I feel sad or down most days. I've got a lot to be thankful for, and I like to focus on the good things. Is that what you were looking for, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you found yourself acting on sudden impulses or making quick decisions without thinking them through lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I wouldn't say I do that. Well, um... I really try to pay attention, I do. I wouldn't say I have trouble, exactly. I mean, if it's a good movie, I can sit through it just fine, mostly. 

Sometimes, though, if a conversation goes on for a little while, or if I'm reading a long article in the paper... well, I suppose my mind might wander just a tiny bit. But I always catch myself right away! I think, "Oh, goodness, pay attention," and I pull myself right back.

I'm not being rude or ignoring anyone on purpose, I promise. I try very hard to be a good listener. That's just a normal part of getting older, right, Doctor?

I guess maybe a little bit? But I think it's pretty normal to notice things going on around you, isn't it? I mean, I like to be aware of my surroundings. \n\nBut, well, sometimes if I'm... say, trying to paint something, or maybe jot down some ideas for a story I've been thinking about... if I hear a loud truck go by outside, or if I suddenly remember a weed I meant to pull in the garden, my mind just kind of... jumps right over to that. And then it takes me a minute to figure out where I was with my painting. \n\nIt's not a huge problem, really! I always get back to it eventually. I just have a curious mind, I suppose. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm too scatterbrained. Does that sound like what you mean, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you felt down, sad, or experienced a low mood most days, even when things seem to be going well for you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't feel that way at all. I try to look on the bright side of things, you know? \n\nI mean, everyone has a gloomy day here and there, sure, but... no, I wouldn't say I feel sad or down most days. I've got a lot to be thankful for, and I like to focus on the good things. I always try to keep a smile on my face, even if things get a little complicated. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't feel down like that. I just try to be a cheerful sort of person. Is that what you were looking for, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you found yourself acting on sudden impulses or making quick decisions without thinking them through lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, I like to think I'm a pretty sensible fellow. \n\nSometimes I might, you know, decide to start a little project around the house on a whim—like rearranging the garage or deciding to bake a pie out of the blue. But that's just keeping active, right? I don't think it's anything reckless or... or bad. I always mean well with whatever I'm doing. \n\nI try to be thoughtful about things, especially at my age. I certainly don't want to cause any trouble or make a mess of things. Does that sound alright to you, Doctor? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling unusually happy, overly confident, or excessively optimistic lately, even if others might not see a reason for it?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I try to be a positive person, like I said before, but I don't think I'm... unusually happy or anything. Just, you know, content. \n\nI certainly don't think I'm overly confident. If anything, I'm just an ordinary guy trying to make the most of his retirement. I don't go around acting like I'm better than anyone else or taking big risks because I think I can't fail. That just wouldn't be very polite or sensible, would it? \n\nI just like to keep busy and stay cheerful. I hope that's normal enough. I'm not giving you the wrong idea, am I, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need less sleep lately—like feeling rested after only a few hours of sleep or feeling energized even if you haven't slept much?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I've always been a fairly good sleeper, thankfully. I mean, at my age, I might wake up a little earlier than I used to, maybe to use the restroom or just because the sun is coming up. But I certainly don't feel like I need *less* sleep. \n\nIf I don't get a good night's rest, I definitely feel it the next day. I get a bit groggy, just like anyone else would. I try to get my solid seven or eight hours, you know, to keep my strength up and stay healthy. \n\nI'm not doing anything strange like staying up all night or running around on two hours of sleep. That just wouldn't be very good for my health, would it? I hope that's the kind of answer you're looking for, Doctor. I'm trying my best to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you found it hard to stop worrying or felt on edge most days since retiring, like you can’t fully relax even when you want to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm on edge or worrying all the time. I mean, sure, retirement is a big change, and sometimes I might wonder if I'm doing enough with my days or if the house is in good order. But I think that's just being responsible, right? \n\nI don't think I'm sitting around worrying in a bad way. I like to keep myself occupied so I don't get too bored, but I can definitely sit down and watch a good television program in the evening. I'm not a nervous wreck or anything like that. \n\nI just want to make sure I'm doing things right and not letting the days slip by. That's a pretty normal way to look at things, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you ever felt an uncomfortable urge to keep moving or like you can't sit still, even when you're not trying to be productive or complete a task?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose sometimes I might get a little fidgety, but nothing too terrible, I promise. It's just... well, for example, if I'm sitting through a quiet meeting, or maybe listening to a long talk at church, I do find it a bit tricky to just sit perfectly still. \n\nI might shift around in my seat a bit, or maybe tap my foot without realizing it. Sometimes I just feel this... this little urge to get up and stretch my legs, even though I know it's not the polite time to do so. I try very hard to be respectful and stay put, of course! \n\nI think maybe it's just my body wanting to stay active, you know? I'm still doing okay overall, I hope.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had trouble staying focused or found your mind wandering during activities that require sustained attention, like reading, watching a movie, or following a conversation?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to pay attention, I do. I wouldn't say I have trouble, exactly. I mean, if it's a good movie, I can sit through it just fine, mostly. \n\nSometimes, though, if a conversation goes on for a little while, or if I'm reading a long article in the paper... well, I suppose my mind might wander just a tiny bit. But I always catch myself right away! I think, \"Oh, goodness, pay attention,\" and I pull myself right back. \n\nI think it's just normal to get a little distracted sometimes, isn't it? Well, um... I know we touched on this a little bit before, and I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is. I'm still doing my best, I promise. But, I suppose... well, if I'm being completely honest, things have gotten just a bit tricky lately. 

Like I mentioned, my wife has had to sort of step in and take over the calendar. She checks behind me a lot now, just to make sure I haven't missed anything important. She's so wonderful about it, but I do feel bad putting that on her. And, um, yes... I have had to apologize to the folks at the community center, and my garden group, too. More than once, I'm afraid. I just... I forget things I promised to do, or I get mixed up, and I end up letting people down. 

I hate doing that, I really do. I don't want anyone to think I'm unreliable or that I don't care about our community. I try so hard to be helpful. It's just a little bump in the road, though, right? Everyone needs a little extra support sometimes... wouldn't you say, Doctor?

Sometimes I just feel this... this little urge to get up and stretch my legs, even though I know it's not the polite time to do so. I try very hard to be respectful and stay put, of course! \n\nI think maybe it's just my body wanting to stay active, you know? A lot of folks my age probably get a little stiff sitting for too long, don't you think? It's not a big bother to anyone else, I hope. That's not too unusual, is it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings or behaviors caused you any real problems in your daily life—like making it hard to enjoy time with family, follow through on plans, or get along with others?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, um... I wouldn't say they cause *real* problems. Nothing too serious, I promise. I try very hard to be dependable! But... well, I suppose I have needed a little extra help lately. \n\nMy wife, bless her, she’s had to take over keeping our calendar and, you know, sort of checking behind me to make sure things are in order. She’s just so organized, so it makes sense for her to handle it, right? \n\nAnd... well, I guess I have had to apologize to the folks at the community center, and my garden group, too. Maybe more than once, if I'm being perfectly honest. Just for, um, letting people down or forgetting things I promised to help with. I always feel just terrible about it when it happens, I really do. I don't want anyone thinking poorly of me, or thinking I don't care. \n\nBut we all need a little help keeping things straight sometimes, don't we, Doctor? I'm still doing okay overall, I hope.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had trouble staying focused or found your mind wandering during activities that require sustained attention, like reading, watching a movie, or following a conversation?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to pay attention, I do. I wouldn't say I have trouble, exactly. I mean, if it's a good movie, I can sit through it just fine, mostly. \n\nSometimes, though, if a conversation goes on for a little while, or if I'm reading a long article in the paper... well, I suppose my mind might wander just a tiny bit. But I always catch myself right away! I think, \"Oh, goodness, pay attention,\" and I pull myself right back. \n\nI think it's just normal to get a little distracted sometimes, isn't it? Especially if the topic isn't terribly exciting. I'm sure everybody's mind drifts now and then. I'm not being rude or ignoring anyone on purpose, I promise. I try very hard to be a good listener. That's just a normal part of getting older, right, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loss of interest in activities or hobbies that you used to enjoy, like painting or spending time with family?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, um... I still love doing the things I've always done. Spending time with my family... well, that's just the best part of my day! And I still very much enjoy my gardening and, you know, just tinkering around the house. \n\nI mean, I might get a little sidetracked while I'm doing those things sometimes, but I certainly haven't lost any interest in them, I promise. I try very hard to stay as active and involved as I can. It's so important to keep your spirits up and stay busy at my age, wouldn't you agree, Doctor? \n\nI really wouldn't want you to think I was feeling down, or... or unappreciative of my family or anything like that. Everything in that department is just fine, I assure you. Well, um... I suppose it hasn't been forever, you know. I mean, I've always been a little scatterbrained here and there, but nothing that really got in the way. 

I guess... well, if I really think about it, it started to become more of a, um, a noticeable thing—a real problem, I suppose you could say—after I retired. That was about a year and two months ago. I used to do handyman work, you see, always working with my hands, moving from one task to the next. It kept me on my toes!

I think a lot of folks probably go through a bit of an adjustment period when they retire, don't they, Doctor? It's just taking me a little longer to find my footing, that's all.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um, yes, I suppose it has been quite a while now. Like I mentioned, it really started to become a... well, a bit of a bother, I guess, right after I retired. And that was, oh, about a year and two months ago now. 

So, yes, definitely longer than six months. I really thought I would have gotten the hang of this whole retirement thing by now, you know? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just sitting around complaining. I'm trying very hard to get back on track, I really am. It's just taking a bit longer than I expected, that's all. That's fairly normal, though, isn't it?

I'm trying my best to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you found it hard to stop worrying or felt on edge most days since retiring, like you can’t fully relax even when you want to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I'm on edge or worrying all the time. I mean, sure, retirement is a big change, and sometimes I might wonder if I'm doing enough with my days or if the house is in good order. But I think that's just being responsible, right? \n\nI don't think I'm sitting around worrying in a bad way. I like to keep myself occupied so I don't get too bored, but I can definitely sit down and watch a good television program in the evening. I'm not a nervous wreck or anything like that. \n\nI just want to make sure I'm doing things right and not letting the days slip by. That's a pretty normal way to look at things, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you ever felt an uncomfortable urge to keep moving or like you can't sit still, even when you're not trying to be productive or complete a task?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose sometimes I might get a little fidgety, but nothing too terrible, I promise. It's just... well, for example, if I'm sitting through a quiet meeting, or maybe listening to a long talk at church, I do find it a bit tricky to just sit perfectly still. \n\nI might shift around in my seat a bit, or maybe tap my foot without realizing it. Sometimes I just feel this... this little urge to get up and stretch my legs, even though I know it's not the polite time to do so. I try very hard to be respectful and stay put, of course! \n\nI think maybe it's just my body wanting to stay active, you know? A lot of folks my age probably get a little stiff sitting for too long, don't you think? It's not a big bother to anyone else, I hope. That's not too unusual, is it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings or behaviors caused you any real problems in your daily life—like making it hard to enjoy time with family, follow through on plans, or get along with others?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, um... I wouldn't say they cause *real* problems. Nothing too serious, I promise. I try very hard to be dependable! But... well, I suppose I have needed a little extra help lately. \n\nMy wife, bless her, she’s had to take over keeping our calendar and, you know, sort of checking behind me to make sure things are in order. She’s just so organized, so it makes sense for her to handle it, right? \n\nAnd... well, I guess I have had to apologize to the folks at the community center, and my garden group, too. Maybe more than once, if I'm being perfectly honest. Just for, um, letting people down or forgetting things I promised to help with. I always feel just terrible about it when it happens, I really do. I don't want anyone thinking poorly of me, or thinking I don't care. \n\nBut we all need a little help keeping things straight sometimes, don't we, Doctor? I'm still doing okay overall, I hope.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had trouble staying focused or found your mind wandering during activities that require sustained attention, like reading, watching a movie, or following a conversation?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to pay attention, I do. I wouldn't say I have trouble, exactly. I mean, if it's a good movie, I can sit through it just fine, mostly. \n\nSometimes, though, if a conversation goes on for a little while, or if I'm reading a long article in the paper... well, I suppose my mind might wander just a tiny bit. But I always catch myself right away! I think, \"Oh, goodness, pay attention,\" and I pull myself right back. \n\nI think it's just normal to get a little distracted sometimes, isn't it? Especially if the topic isn't terribly exciting. I'm sure everybody's mind drifts now and then. I'm not being rude or ignoring anyone on purpose, I promise. I try very hard to be a good listener. That's just a normal part of getting older, right, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loss of interest in activities or hobbies that you used to enjoy, like painting or spending time with family?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, um... I still love doing the things I've always done. Spending time with my family... well, that's just the best part of my day! And I still very much enjoy my gardening and, you know, just tinkering around the house. \n\nI mean, I might get a little sidetracked while I'm doing those things sometimes, but I certainly haven't lost any interest in them, I promise. I try very hard to stay as active and involved as I can. It's so important to keep your spirits up and stay busy at my age, wouldn't you agree, Doctor? \n\nI really wouldn't want you to think I was feeling down, or... or unappreciative of my family or anything like that. Everything in that department is just fine, I assure you. I'm still quite a happy fellow overall!\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have these experiences led to any noticeable difficulties in your ability to function well in social situations, at community events, or in managing your personal responsibilities at home?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know we touched on this a little bit before, and I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is. I'm still doing my best, I promise. But, I suppose... well, if I'm being completely honest, things have gotten just a bit tricky lately. \n\nLike I mentioned, my wife has had to sort of step in and take over the calendar. She checks behind me a lot now, just to make sure I haven't missed anything important. She's so wonderful about it, but I do feel bad putting that on her. And, um, yes... I have had to apologize to the folks at the community center, and my garden group, too. More than once, I'm afraid. I just... I forget things I promised to do, or I get mixed up, and I end up letting people down. \n\nI hate doing that, I really do. I don't want anyone to think I'm unreliable or that I don't care about our community. I try so hard to be helpful. It's just a little bump in the road, though, right? Everyone needs a little extra support sometimes... wouldn't you say, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these feelings or behaviors that we've been talking about?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it hasn't been forever, you know. I mean, I've always been a little scatterbrained here and there, but nothing that really got in the way. \n\nI guess... well, if I really think about it, it started to become more of a, um, a noticeable thing—a real problem, I suppose you could say—after I retired. That was about a year and two months ago. I used to do handyman work, you see, always working with my hands, moving from one task to the next. It kept me on my toes! \n\nBut once I stopped working and the days got a little quieter... well, that's when things got a bit more jumbled up. I hope that makes sense? I think a lot of folks probably go through a bit of an adjustment period when they retire, don't they, Doctor? It's just taking me a little longer to find my footing, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been present for at least 6 months or more?\n\nPatient: Well, um, yes, I suppose it has been quite a while now. Like I mentioned, it really started to become a... well, a bit of a bother, I guess, right after I retired. And that was, oh, about a year and two months ago now. \n\nSo, yes, definitely longer than six months. I really thought I would have gotten the hang of this whole retirement thing by now, you know? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just sitting around complaining. I'm trying very hard to get back on track, I really am. It's just taking a bit longer than I expected, that's all. That's fairly normal, though, isn't it?"
